Brooklyn Building Collapse Leaves 8 Dead as Crews Race to Search for Survivors

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

A three-story building in Brooklyn's Flatbush neighborhood collapsed Wednesday afternoon, killing at least eight and leaving 17 people injured.

At a press conference Wednesday night, FDNY Commissioner Lillian Bonsignore said that seven of the casualties were children, but did not specify whether those children were among the dead or injured.

More than 250 firefighters along with a canine unit and search drones were on the scene at 1321 Flatbush Avenue combing through debris to search for survivors on Thursday morning. Roads around the collapsed building have been closed to traffic.

Part of the wall of 1323 Flatbush also came down in the collapse, leaving apartments in that building exposed to the elements. Neighbors were evacuated from the property and brought to the nearby Flatbush YMCA as a precaution.

The collapsed building at 1321 Flatbush, in Brooklyn's Little Haiti neighborhood, was built in 1930 and had four apartment units on the second and third floors while the first floor was a commercial storefront.

At the time of the collapse workers were excavating a lot adjacent to the property at 2625 Bedford Avenue. Buildings Commissioner Ahmed Tigani said that the Bedford lot had been approved for a three story, 64-unit building but another permit application had been put in for a nine-story building on the site. A stop work order was issued for that project.

1321 Flatbush Avenue has 98 recorded violations according to the city's department of Housing, Preservation, and Development dating back to 1972. Eleven of those violations are from this year alone, and include "unsafe electrical wiring" and a notice to "provide an adequate supply of water for the wet sprinkler and/or standpipe."

The Brooklyn District Attorney’s office, alongside the Department of Buildings and local law enforcement, has launched an active joint inquiry to determine whether any criminal negligence or building code violations contributed to the disaster.

New York Mayor Zohran Mamdani said in a statement: “There are no words for a loss of this magnitude. My heart is with the families and loved ones of those we have lost, those who are injured and everyone grieving today.“

She previously worked at Business Insider as an executive editor covering news, entertainment, and digital culture, and was the company's founding Singapore bureau chief. She was also a senior editor at BuzzFeed, covering lifestyle, fashion, and beauty. Plant roots have a clever way to tell good and bad fungi apart, even though they both give off the exact same molecules

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Good and bad fungi release the exact same chemical signal but plant roots still know which one is dangerous

A fungus growing on a crop sounds like bad news, but not all fungi are harmful. Plants also rely on helpful fungi to take up nutrients through their roots.

Attackers and partners alike are built from the same material, a sugar chain called chitin. Fragments of it end up in the soil whenever a fungus grows or breaks down.

A new study shows how a receptor on legume roots sorts those fragments by length.

Receptors tightly grip long chitin chains, the kind that come with a fungal attack, and raise the alarm. Short ones, the kind that partner fungi shed, barely register.

Kira Gysel and Kasper Røjkjær Andersen of the Department of Molecular Biology and Genetics at Aarhus University in Denmark led the work.

Simon Boje Hansen and Henriette Rübsam are the first authors, with co-authors at the University of Copenhagen and the French research center CERMAV. The experiments were done in two small legumes used as laboratory models, not in crops.

In an interview with Earth.com, Gysel said the puzzle was that chitin looks the same along its whole length.

“It’s the same sugar repeated, like a string of identical beads, with nothing to tell one part of the chain from another,” she said.

Chitin is the main building block of a fungal cell wall. When a fungus grows or gets broken down, pieces of the chain of different lengths end up in the soil. Roots read these fragments of fungi to keep track of the microbes around them.

Long fragments usually come with an attack and, in legumes, they set off a much stronger defense than short ones. Short fragments come with the helpful fungi that plug into roots and exchange nutrients with the plant.

How a root told the two apart was the open question.

The team produced the outer part of a root receptor called CHIP13 and tested how hard it holds chitin chains of different lengths. It held an eight-sugar chain more than 1,000 times more tightly than a five-sugar chain.

Then they froze the receptor into crystals with the long chain attached and mapped it, atom by atom.

The receptor holds the chain at three points. Two are standard chitin-binding pockets found in related receptors. The third sits between them, and the team named it the bridge domain, because it spans the middle of the chain.

Gysel told Earth.com that the structure answered the question at a glance.

“CHIP13 holds the chain at three main contact points, and the central one, the bridge domain, is what coordinates binding of the long chitin chain,” Gysel said.

“Only chains long enough to reach all three are held tightly and can activate the plant’s immune response. It’s really a molecular measuring tape.”

Swapping out two amino acids near the bridge cut the receptor’s grip 100-fold. In a second legume, barrel medic, mutant plants given a version of the matching receptor with the same two swaps didn’t regain their response to chitin.

Legume roots also need a second receptor, CERK6, to respond to chitin, and CERK6 can barely hold chitin at all. Boje Hansen said that had been a puzzle.

“It turns out the two receptors split the work: one recognizes the signal, the other passes it on. Neither can do the job alone,” he said.

In leaf cells engineered to carry both receptors, CHIP13 pulled in CERK6 only once chitin was bound. The weakened version of CHIP13 didn’t recruit it at all.

The team’s model is a pair of CHIP13 receptors holding chitin and recruiting two CERK6 partners, which then pass the alarm into the cell.

To test the receptors in a living plant without altering its genes, the team raised nanobodies – tiny antibody-like proteins made by immunizing a llama – that plug each receptor’s chitin-binding site.

They soaked intact seedlings of the model legume Lotus japonicus in them. When asked by Earth.com what else about the research stood out, Gysel pointed to that experiment.

“We simply applied them to plants from the outside, and we weren’t sure it would work at all,” she said. “We thought the plant cell wall would block the nanobodies from reaching the receptors on the surface of the plant cells.”

The nanobodies reached the receptors. Seedlings treated with the CERK6 blocker responded to chitin about as weakly as plants missing that gene, and the CHIP13 blocker cut the response sharply too.

Both legumes are laboratory models, and the measuring tape hasn’t been checked in a crop. The bridge domain can be predicted in many related receptors across higher plants, but a predicted domain isn’t a tested one.

Gysel said the two receptors are only a glimpse of a larger immune system. Plants use many related receptors to monitor soil microbes. How those receptors work together, and whether others read signals by length, is what her group wants to find out next.

The stakes she described are agricultural. Strengthening the chitin alarm could make crops more resistant to fungal disease, but only if it doesn’t also fire at the fungi the roots depend on.

“Knowing how the long chitin chains are read helps us improve crops without tipping the balance, so we don’t weaken their defenses to make room for helpful fungi, or the other way around,” Gysel said.

Which crop receptors carry a bridge domain, and whether they measure chitin the same way, is the next thing to check.

The full study was published in the journal Science.

Lego’s BrickLink marketplace has been offline for a week following ‘unusual activity’

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

BrickLink was taken offline on November 5th but Lego doesn’t know when it will return.

BrickLink was taken offline on November 5th but Lego doesn’t know when it will return.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Bricklink, a popular online marketplace that Lego bought in 2019 where fans can buy and sell parts and sets, has been down for a week and the company doesn’t know when it will return. The team who runs the site has been sharing updates over the past week including estimates of when it will be back up. It was last expected to return on November 11th, according to Jay’s Brick Blog, but a statement Lego shared to the site yesterday says there’s currently no estimate of when BrickLink will be back online.

Lego hasn’t explained exactly what happened with the marketplace, but says it “took BrickLink offline after identifying unusual activity on the platform. Our immediate priority was to secure the site, protect our members and ensure the integrity of BrickLink.” There are also no specifics on when BrickLink will return, as Lego works to contain what happened. “Before bringing the site back online, we need to fully understand what has happened. That means containing the issue, investigating how it happened and checking for related issues.”

Three years ago, BrickLink experienced a similar issue around the same time of year resulting in the marketplace being offline for several days due to a cybersecurity incident. Lego shut down the site “out of an abundance of caution” after receiving a “threat and ransom demand.” When BrickLink returned, users were prompted to change their passwords with stronger requirements, but were assured their accounts had not been compromised during the incident.

Jensen and Lori Huang's Nvidia fortune is jointly controlled. How that makes things complicated now that he's resigned

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

A version of this article appeared in CNBC's Inside Wealth newsletter, a weekly guide to the high-net-worth investor and consumer. Sign up to receive future editions straight to your inbox.

Jensen Huang's $140 billion stake in Nvidia is largely held by trusts that are co-owned with his wife, Lori, creating added uncertainty for the shares and his financial future following his resignation from the company, according to attorneys and SEC filings.

Huang and his wife jointly own about 800 million Nvidia shares worth roughly $135 billion through a complex web of trusts and LLCs created for tax and estate planning purposes, according to SEC filings. Jensen Huang directly owns about 70 million shares valued at about $12 billion.

While owning company stock through a trust is common among tech founders and CEOs, it's less typical for a founder to have equal ownership with a spouse, tax experts say. The arrangement further complicates Huang's sudden exit from the company, but could prove to be a stabilizing force for the shares, since the trusts make it more difficult for either Huang or his wife to quickly sell.

"Each of Mr. Huang and his wife may be deemed to have shared beneficial ownership of, and to exercise shared voting and investment power over," according to an SEC filing.

According to the filings, Jensen and Lori Huang own about 530 million shares as co-trustees of the Jen-Hsun and Lori Huang Living Trust, created in 1995. They're also co-trustees of the Huang 2012 Irrevocable Trust, which owns 31.4 million shares, and the Huang Irrevocable Remainder Trust, which owns 109 million shares. About 135 million shares are held by LLCs that have the Living Trust as the sole member, according to the filings.

Lori Huang is the president of the Jen-Hsun and Lori Huang Foundation, which owns 69 million shares and reported $9 billion in net assets in 2024, according to IRS filings.

Each of the trust structures serves different tax or estate purposes, according to trust and estate attorneys. The living trust is likely a revocable marital trust, since it was created 10 years after the Huangs married and two years after Nvidia was founded in 1993.  Attorneys say living trusts help couples avoid the potentially expensive and public probate process  when one spouse dies. Many couples, for example, create living trusts for homes they purchase or other assets.

The main tax benefit of living trusts is that when one spouse dies, the surviving spouse gets a step-up in basis for the entire trust's assets, rather than just their half, according to attorneys.

While the rules of each living trust vary, attorneys say shares in the living trust can typically be sold or divided fairly easily in the case of a divorce.

"It's like a joint checking account," said Kathleen Martone, partner at Varnum LLP.

While the revocable trust would typically allow each of them to sell their shares, lawyers said it is common for executives or their trusts to have agreements with the company limiting the sale or transfer of shares to prevent large-scale liquidation.

"In the event they divide shares in their revocable trust, there's probably transfer restrictions. He may have to hold them for her benefit, and she would get the monetary benefit of the shares but not voting or management rights," said Leslie Evans, partner at Withers. "He could retain the stock while Lori receives other assets or an equalization payment."

The more complicated structures used by the Huangs, and the ones with the largest tax benefits, are the irrevocable trusts. The SEC filings state that "Mr Huang and his wife are co-trustees" of both trusts. With an irrevocable trust, the creator transfers ownership of an asset into the trust and the terms can't be changed without the consent of the beneficiaries or a court.

Attorneys say the 2012 irrevocable trust is likely an "intentionally defective grantor trust," a type of irrevocable trust where the creator gifts assets but is still treated by the IRS as the owner of the property for income tax purposes.

The structure allows the creator to lend money to the trust to buy the shares, as well as pay income taxes for the trust when it earns money. The result — an unlimited gift to the trust that escapes the estate tax.

What's more, the trust can avoid capital gains taxes on any appreciation of the shares in the trust.

"The trust principal is staying high, my own taxable personal assets are being used to pay the tax, and I'm able to funnel more and more money into the trust," said Azriel Baer, trust and estates partner at Farrell Fritz, P.C. in New York.

Their other irrevocable trust, the "Huang Irrevocable Remainder Trust," was likely created for the benefit of their children.

By definition, the trustees and terms of an irrevocable trust are difficult to change. Since Jensen Huang and Lori Huang are both co-trustees, neither can likely remove or sell the Nvidia shares held by the trust, experts say.

"An irrevocable trust is its own entity that has its own operational provisions, and so it has some estate and asset protection planning," Martone said. "Theoretically an irrevocable trust would be protected from a divorcing spouse as a creditor."

Of course, the protections all depend on the terms of the trust, attorneys say. Whether an irrevocable trust can be changed or "decanted" in legal parlance depends in part on state law. California, where the Huangs live, is strict when it comes to changing irrevocable trusts.

For Nvidia shareholders, the irrevocable trusts could provide stability for the shares, since it would be difficult for either Jensen or Lori Huang to remove the shares from the trust and sell them.

Attorneys say it's unlikely the Huangs would change a trust created for the benefit of their children.

"If (the trusts) are for benefit for kids, there's no reason to pull them back into their estate," said Rochelle Schultz, shareholder at Weinstock Manion.

Denzel Washington And Robert Pattinson Are Doing A New Movie Together. One Thing Surprised The Dune Star

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Someone Asked Timothèe Chalamet How He Feels About Dune’s Ending (And Now I Can't Wait)

Zendaya And Robert Pattinson’s Takes On Shooting A Dune Movie In The Desert Have Me Sweating

Robert Pattinson is living out a real-life ‘pinch me’ moment, and it has nothing to do with Batman. The actor has always wanted to work with Denzel Washington, who is one of his acting heroes. Well, that dream is coming true, because Washington and Pattinson are doing a movie together. However, there is one thing that surprised the Dune star.

Pattinson's first movie of the 2027 movie schedule, The Take, was an easy yes thanks to a “dream-team situation,” partnering him with one of the greatest actors of all time. The Primetime actor dished with Entertainment Weekly about what caught him off guard (in the best way) while working with Washington:

Denzel is one of the reasons I wanted to become an actor, so getting to work with him was pretty incredible. What I loved was seeing how alive the material is for him. Even after all these years of acting, he’s still looking for something new in every take. I also didn’t realize how much he liked to improvise, which made it all the more exciting. You’d start a five-page scene thinking you knew where it was going, and then suddenly you’re completely off-piste. It was really thrilling.

Sounds like Pattinson had to stay on his toes just as much as his character, Noah, who is recruited by “a charismatic thief” (aka Denzel Washington) to rob Manhattan’s richest bank. To make things more complicated, this is also the bank where his girlfriend (played by Daisy Edgar-Jones) works as a teller. The movie’s official synopsis teases that “a bank guard, a teller, and a master thief get caught in a deadly game of cons and double crosses.” This is definitely up the actor’s alley, as he tends to lean toward twisty movies, such as Primetime about To Catch A Predator host Chris Hansen.

However, this movie sounds less polarizing and more thrilling. I’m sure some of that improv worked in Pattinson’s favor given the movie’s dynamics. It’s being described as a mix of a heist film and psychological drama, and promises to keep you surprised until the end. In fact, director Fernando Meirelles assures that even the last 10 seconds are important. Part of the excitement of the movie is being credited to Washington. Meirelles backs Pattinson’s statements and shares how easy improvising is for The Equalizer actor:

He constantly surprises you. Sometimes, when I ask him where a particular line came from, he’ll simply say, ‘I have no idea.’ There are quite a few of those moments in the final cut.

While the movie is strictly from Pattinson’s POV, it sounds like Washington is going to be a scene-stealer. However, while Washington is getting rave reviews from his coworkers, the director also made sure to shout out Pattinson, calling him “one of the best actors working today.” This is definitely true, given the actor has released three movies this year and still has Dune: Part 3 set to hit the 2026 schedule on December 18. He certainly doesn’t seem to be slowing down, and I wonder if Washington gave him any advice on working so much.

Though this full-circle moment with the Gladiator II actor had to be a highlight, given Pattinson previously worked with Denzel's son John David Washington in Christopher Nolan’s Tenet. Maybe one day the three of them can do a project together. Perhaps something just as ‘puzzling’ as The Take and with just as much improvising.

Hopefully, when the movie comes out on January 15, 2027, they’ll share what lines were improvised, because I’m dying to know. At least, I’ll be able to watch it with a Netflix subscription.

The Red Devils find themselves in the relegation zone after just one win in ten Premier League games so far this season, and it makes sense that they’ve decided to replace Carrick with Pochettino.

Man buys two-bedroom apartment for just $1.70 in China’s Black Friday-style sale

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

A man in China has purchased an apartment for just $1.70 during a shopping promotion event, and it’s just as absurd as it sounds.

Shopping sales usually mean saving money on a shiny new TV or picking up a discounted game or two, but one lucky man in China took money-saving to an entirely new level by purchasing a two-bedroom apartment for just $1.70.

The property was sold through Chinese bidding platform Alibaba Auction as part of the country’s Double 11 event, an annual shopping festival that’s similar to Black Friday.

Located in Heilongjiang province in northeastern China, the seventh-floor apartment measures 68 square metres (roughly 732 square feet) and has two bedrooms.

According to the South China Morning Post, bidding started at one yuan (around $0.15) and was capped at 11 yuan (around $1.65). While 1,677 bidders took part, it was an anonymous shopper from eastern China’s Jiangsu-Zhejiang region who managed to secure the property.

Despite landing a new home for less than a cup of coffee, the buyer revealed he has no plans to relocate. Instead, he intends to use the two-bedroom property as a place to stay during winter holidays.

While the man may have purchased the apartment for $1.70, he will need to pay around 2,000 yuan (approximately $300) in deed taxes to complete the title transfer. SCMP also cited a warning from Beijing Jinsu Law Firm, which said buyers considering ultra-cheap properties should check for issues such as unclear ownership, unpaid taxes, and outstanding fees.

Either way, securing an apartment for just $1.70 puts it right next to owning an entire Japanese castle for $64,000 (yes, this is a real thing).

Nvidia’s Jensen Huang resigns, chipmaker names CFO Colette Kress as interim CEO

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Jensen Huang has resigned from his position as president and chief executive of Nvidia, the company said on Thursday, just a few days after he admitted to a romantic affair with an alleged Chinese spy, sparking a U.S. national security crisis.

In a regulatory filing, Nvidia said its board accepted Huang’s resignation on Wednesday.

Nvidia said its current chief financial officer and executive vice president, Collete Kress, will take over as interim CEO until the company finds a permanent successor. Kress will continue to serve as the company’s CFO.

Nvidia’s shares were down nearly 12% in early morning trading on Thursday. Since Friday, the company’s stock has fallen over 19%, resulting in a market cap loss of more than $855 billion.

Huang founded Nvidia in 1993 and has led it as CEO ever since. Under his leadership, Nvidia played a crucial role in developing the the graphics processing unit (GPU), a specialized chip that does graphics processing for PCs, as we know it today. The chips were not an immediate hit, and the early days of Nvidia were characterized by struggle and layoffs, but eventually, it struck on a GPU design that performed so well, it became the standard for graphics processing.

These chips also turned out to be extremely good at doing large numbers of calculations simultaneously, which has made them popular for computation-intensive tasks like crypto mining, and more recently, training and running neural networks, which are what AI models are at their core.

Nvidia was initially focused on making GPUs aimed at gaming, but it had already moved into general-purpose computing by 2006 with the launch of the CUDA architecture, which lets developers use the chips for other tasks, such as doing calculations and research. After AI researchers began using the company’s GPUs to train neural networks over a decade ago, Huang leaned in towards developing chips for AI and then data centers, and grew it from there into its dominant position.

The potential implications of Huang’s departure for the AI and data center industries are vast, given how active Huang, and by extension Nvidia, has been in furthering the AI boom. In particular, it raises questions about the future of Nvidia’s sprawling AI investments, which have helped bankroll everything from startups developing new technologies to the enormous data centers needed to run them.

Under Huang, Nvidia deftly used its financial muscle to support companies buying its chips, effectively helping to finance the market for its own products, as well as lobbying Washington to relax potential rules around AI and data center regulation.

As of the end of its second quarter, Nvidia reported $99 billion worth of investments and another $25 billion of commitments. It also has $36 billion in cloud service commitments, which are arrangements that help AI cloud operators finance the GPUs and data center equipment they buy from Nvidia, with the chipmaker saying it will buy compute from them and potentially earn a share of their operators’ revenue.

Nvidia has also been investing directly in neoclouds, the companies serving a lot of the AI processing needs. In January, the company invested $2 billion in CoreWeave, and another $2 billion in Nebius in March. The chipmaker was said to be considering being a guarantor for Nscale’s planned West Virginia data center project. It’s also signed up to provide up to $105 billion worth of credit guarantees for an OpenAI data center campus in Ohio.

Nvidia’s also a major investor in the labs building the AI models that run on its GPUs, putting $30 billion into OpenAI, and $10 billion into Anthropic. It’s also backed Mira Murati’s Thinking Machines Lab as part of a deal involving at least 1 GW of its Vera Rubin GPU systems. The chipmarker has even invested $3.5 billion in MediaTek to help the Taiwanese company develop custom AI chips that work with Nvidia’s systems.

Investors were already uneasy with the enormous amounts of debt companies in the space were raising to sustain the AI infrastructure buildout, and Huang’s exit throws another uncertainty-shaped wrench into the works. CoreWeave’s shares were down about 9% on Thursday, reversing part of a 25% rally on Wednesday, while Nebius is down about 16%. IREN fell 6.6%.

Nvidia’s board has also appointed Harvey Jones, a managing partner of Square Wave Ventures and former CEO of Synopsys, as the company’s first-ever chair of the board of directors. Jones has been on Nvidia’s board since 1993.

Huang is the single largest personal shareholder at Nvidia, but he owns only about 3.5% of the company’s shares. And, unlike most of Silicon Valley’s tech giants that were founded far later, he does not hld supervoting rights. That means that the board had the power to vote him out at any time.

Huang famously ran a very flat organization, with around 60 direct reports. There was no heir apparent or succession plan either (although two of his children also worked in executive roles at the company). Many pundits described his tight control over the company he founded as: Huang is Nvidia/Nvidia his Huang, as if the two were inseparable. However, that is clearly not the case and we’ll have to watch to see how Nvidia finds a path forward without him.

The industry will get its first glimpse of that next week during the quarterly conference call. Nvidia reports earnings on Tuesday.

“Jensen’s contributions to NVIDIA are profound. NVIDIA is one of the most consequential technology companies in the world. The Board and the company thank Jensen for his immeasurable contributions to the company and the industry,” the company said in a statement.

Jensen Huang resigns as Nvidia CEO after 33 years, effective immediately

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

NVIDIA founder and CEO Jensen Huang at NVIDIA GTC 2026 in San Jose

Jensen Huang has resigned as Nvidia’s president, CEO, and board member, effective immediately, the company’s board announced today, ending a 33-year run in which he built the chipmaker he co-founded into the roughly $4T centrepiece of the AI boom.

Chief financial officer Colette Kress steps in as interim CEO while the board searches for a permanent successor, and Harvey C. Jones becomes the company’s first-ever board chair, a role Huang never relinquished until now.

“NVIDIA’s strength has always come from its people, and together with our leadership team, I am committed to building on the extraordinary foundation Jensen created over the last 33 years,” Kress said.

The resignation caps a two-week unravelling that began with reports that US intelligence believed Alice Huang, a Megaspeed executive with whom Huang had a relationship, was recruited by China’s Ministry of State Security to spy on him, per the New York Times.

Jensen Huang admitted in a memo to employees that he had been unfaithful to his wife, and on Sunday the board opened an independent investigation while expressing confidence in his leadership.

Yet, that confidence lasted four days, as on Wednesday the NYT reported a second relationship, with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia’s vice president of healthcare and one of his direct reports; the Wall Street Journal reported the company was preparing to fire him; and the board met late into the night without announcing a decision.

By Thursday morning in the US, Huang had resigned.

The market damage is already historic. Nvidia had shed roughly $600B in value since the investigation was announced, and the stock fell as much as 10% more on news of the resignation. The company reports quarterly earnings on November 17, its first ever without the leather-jacketed founder who fronted every one.

The board’s statement does not mention the allegations at all, thanking Huang instead for his “immeasurable contributions”.

He departs holding about 3.6% of the company, and leaves behind the succession vacuum he created: dozens of direct reports, no anointed heir, and an interim CEO drawn from finance rather than the technology roadmap.

Bloomberg reported this week that an outside firm is compiling candidates; prediction markets have favoured Ian Buck, who runs the data-centre business that supplies the AI industry.

Whoever gets the job inherits a company that is simultaneously the most valuable enterprise on Earth, the hinge of the US-China technology conflict, and, as of today, proof that no founder is bigger than his board.

Après 7 défaites en 10 matchs de Premier League, c'est désormais officiel : Crystal Palace vire Pierre Sage !

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Moins de cinq mois après sa nomination à Crystal Palace, Pierre Sage a été officiellement démis de ses fonctions ce jeudi 12 novembre 2026. L'entraîneur français de 47 ans paie un début de saison désastreux en Premier League, laissant le club londonien à la dernière place du championnat.

Pierre Sage n'aura pas résisté au revers concédé à domicile contre Liverpool (1-3), le dimanche 8 novembre 2026 à Selhurst Park, signant une quatrième défaite de rang en championnat. En dix journées de Premier League sous les ordres de Pierre Sage, le bilan comptable est accablant avec 7 défaites, 1 match nul et seulement 2 victoires, soit 7 petits points pris sur 30 possibles. Crystal Palace occupe ainsi la 20e et dernière place du classement, à deux longueurs du premier non-relégable. Une situation critique.

La situation n'était guère plus brillante sur la scène continentale. En Ligue Europa, les Eagles pointent au 21e rang de la phase de ligue avec une seule victoire en quatre rencontres. Au total, Pierre Sage n'aura dirigé que 16 matches sur le banc de Crystal Palace toutes compétitions confondues avant son licenciement.

Sur le terrain, la méthode du coach français n'a jamais fonctionné. L'application stricte d'un bloc haut a constamment exposé son arrière-garde, faisant de Crystal Palace la pire défense d'Angleterre avec 24 buts encaissés en 10 matches de championnat. Le mandat de Pierre Sage a notamment été marqué par une lourde défaite (5-0) subie en octobre 2026 face au rival historique Brighton. Un naufrage tactique.

Toutefois, l'effectif a également pâti des départs estivaux non compensés de Maxence Lacroix et Marc Guéhi en défense centrale, dans un contexte de recrutement restreint par une direction plus économe. La succession d'Oliver Glasner s'annonçait d'ailleurs périlleuse, l'Autrichien étant devenu l'idole des supporters après ses victoires en FA Cup et au Community Shield en 2025, puis en Ligue Conférence, avant de rejoindre Nottingham Forest.

Cette éviction intervient alors que Pierre Sage sortait d'une saison brillante au RC Lens, couronnée du titre de meilleur entraîneur de Ligue 1, d'une victoire en Coupe de France et d'une 2e place en championnat. Selon L'Équipe, le technicien pourrait rebondir sans tarder dans le Nord. Il figure parmi les pistes explorées par les dirigeants artésiens, aux côtés de Didier Digard et Thiago Motta, pour succéder à Yannick Cahuzac, limogé en début de semaine.

De son côté, Crystal Palace négocie activement avec l'entraîneur danois Thomas Frank pour reprendre l'équipe. Les Eagles tenteront de se relancer lors de leur prochain match, programmé le samedi 21 novembre 2026 à 15h00 face à Coventry City à la Coventry Building Society Arena, pour le compte de la 11e journée de Premier League.

Nvidia CEO Has Officially Resigned

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Effective immediately, Jensen Huang has resigned as Nvidia CEO, president, and board member after it was discovered that he was having an affair with a Chinese spy and a company executive.

Financial Times reports that chief financial officer Colette Kress will uphold Huang's position in the interim while the board searches for a replacement, considering both internal and external candidates.

Nvidia's strength has always come from its people, and together with our leadership team, I am committed to building on the extraordinary foundation Jensen created over the last 33 years—Colette Kress, interim CEO.

"Kress has served as Executive Vice President and Chief Financial Officer of Nvidia since joining the company in September 2013," Nvidia said in its official statement. "During her tenure, she has led the company’s finance organization and has played a key role in Nvidia's growth and evolution. Prior to joining Nvidia, Kress spent nearly 25 years in finance roles at leading technology companies, including Cisco and Microsoft.

"Jensen’s contributions to Nvidia are profound. Nvidia is one of the most consequential technology companies in the world. The Board and the company thank Jensen for his immeasurable contributions to the company and the industry."

Jensen Huang was under investigation by the board for his extramarital relationship with Alice Huang, who the US Intelligence identified as a Chinese spy. However, Nvidia does not believe that national security or company secrets were compromised by Jensen Huang's relationship with Alice Huang.

The New York Times later reported on November 11 that Jensen Huang was also entangled with a company executive who reported directly to him—48-year-old vice president of health care, Kimberly Powell.

Nvidia's CEO wants us all to lighten up about AI, because of course he does.

Jensen Huang has admitted that he was "unfaithful" to his wife, although Chinese officials reject claims of espionage. Global Times former chief editor Hu Xijin described the allegations as "a calculated move by the Democratic Party and US establishment to take down Jensen Huang, and by extension, go after Trump."

Following Huang's resignation, Nvidia shares have fallen a further 13%, adding to an already steep decline of around 25% from their October peak amid the investigation into his affairs and a broader technology sell-off following the Democrats' midterm victory. In the last month alone, Nvidia has lost approximately $1.8 trillion in market capitalization.

The future is looking bleak for PC components.

Station to Station coming to PS5, Switch 2 on November 26

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Publisher Engine Software and developer Galaxy Grove will release voxel art railway puzzle game Station to Station for PlayStation 5 and Switch 2 on November 26 for $17.99 / €17.99, the companies announced. Pre-orders are available now via Nintendo eShop with a 20 percent-off launch discount.

Station to Station first launched for PC via Steam on October 2, 2023.

Here is an overview of the game, via Engine Software:

Station to Station is a minimalist and relaxing game about building railway connections and bringing a voxel world to life through the magic of trains. Build your network, watch your surroundings flourish, and enjoy a peaceful journey inspired by titles such as Mini Metro, Train Valley, Dorfromantik, Terra Nil, and Islanders.

In Station to Station, your world starts small with a handful of rural structures. Place stations and create connections to foster growth and expansion. As you build more connections, your area will grow, transforming into a vibrant, lush environment full of life and color! To keep you on your toes, the game also features engaging puzzle elements, challenging you to optimize routes and solve logistical conundrums.

Hop on board and steam through colorful biomes. Breathe life into the dry desert sands, and transform the untamed mountain terrain. Build and watch your tranquil world come to life with an ever-widening tapestry of flora and fauna!

For those eager to put their management and planning skills to the test, each level offers optional challenges, stack bonuses, and unique mechanics tied to every biome! Whether you seek relaxation or mental stimulation, ‘Station to Station’ has something for everyone!

Noga Erez Announces Fourth Album, ‘Duck Season,’ Drops Dark Pop Banger Single ‘People, I Hope You Know the World Is Ending’

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

The Israeli rapper/singer's LP is due out on Feb. 26 and she will hit the road for an extensive North American and European tour in the spring.

Noga Erez has seen the future and it is bleak. But it also has a great, catchy beat. The Tel Aviv, Israel-based singer/rapper laments the spiraling madness of modern life her new single, “People, I hope you know the world is ending,” the first taste of her upcoming fourth studio album, Duck Season. Explore Noga Erez See latest videos, charts and news

With her signature wit and laconic vocal style, Erez lists the litany of dumpster fires in the rapped verses on the song on which she laments, “They never stopped delivering all the boxes/ That’s the one thing/ They got you to step outside and see what the bombs did/ On God,” over a loping beat. Related Anyma Will Return to the Great Pyramids of Giza In 2027 Ebro & Peter Rosenberg Aren't Buying Ye's 'Comeback' Narrative After Antisemitism: 'It's All Marketing' Yella Beezy Found Guilty in 2020 Murder of Rapper MO3 on a Dallas Highway

She continues cleverly weighing in on the various conflicts in our personal and collective lives with the lines: “Let’s weigh the options/ I think what it comes down to is we do Prozac or chocolate, right?/ You don’t gotta wait for a siren to start runnin’/ You ran, I ran/ Wait, should we just send Baron?/ If we want a happy ending we could just a war with Thailand/ Send the Mossad to get a massage/ Hands up if you were on the island!”

The song then bursts into its soaring, uniting chorus, on which Erez sings, “People, I hope you know the world is ending/ Oh yeah/ Don’t you look so sad/ Oh no/ I hope you know the world is ending/ Oh yeah/ But we’re going down together.”

Duck Season is due out on Feb. 26 through Neon Gold/Atlantic Records and, according to a release announcing the project it, “turns the absurdity of modern life into a sprawling, technicolor spectacle, taking aim at a world where outrage is instantaneous and everyone seems to be fair game. The album’s title takes on the darker implications of open season, a time when shooting first has become instinctive and asking questions is almost beside the point. By the closing track, the target has grown even wider: humanity itself is in the crosshairs.”

The new 14-track LP was recorded alongside Erez’s longtime creative and life partner producer/songwriter Ori Rousso and it promises to take the singer’s fans into a brightly colored world where “candy-coated pop hooks collide with bass-heavy hip-hop, snarling guitars, bursts of brass and even spaghetti-Western drama. Military-style chants and cinematic flourishes add to the album’s larger-than-life atmosphere, while Erez moves effortlessly between swagger and moments of startling vulnerability.”

The album is the follow-up to 2024’s The Vandalist and it features collaborations with Iranian rapper Hichkas and singer/model Cara Delevingne.

Erez will spend the spring and summer on the road on a 2027 North American and European tour slated to kick off on March 10 in Phoenix and run through a July 8 show at Nos Alive in Lisbon, Portugal. An artist presale will begin on Nov. 17 at 10 a.m. local time, with fans encouraged to sign up here. A general onsale will follow on Nov. 20 at 10 a.m. local time.

‘A club I like’: Andy Woodman quizzed on Crawley Town managerial post as search for new boss continues

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Andy Woodman was sacked by Bromley last month following a 4-0 defeat to Leicester City at home. The dismissal came as a surprise for many at Hayes Lane and beyond, given the remarkable job Woodman had done in his five years in charge.

Crawley Town are currently without a manager after they sacked Colin Kazim-Richards in October. And, Woodman has been quizzed on the vacancy and whether it is one he would be interested in.

Speaking on BBC Sport’s 72+ EFL podcast, Woodman said: “Crawley’s a club that is very close to me. My son [Freddie Woodman] went on loan. I’ve grown up around the area. It’s a football club I’ve got a lot of time for.

“They have got some big games coming up against teams around them, and if they can climb out of the bottom, they can start getting that club back to where they should be. I do hope they do, because it’s a club I do like.”

Former Bromley manager Woodman will rightly feel hard done by when it comes to his sacking. With this, he will want to prove the decision-makers at the Ravens that they were incorrect to fire him.

Woodman could have the opportunity to return to management with Crawley Town at the bottom of the table, and a big task would await him if he did take the job. But, if the 55-year-old returns to the dugout and gets it right with the Red Devils, it would be another feather in his cap.

Crawley only narrowly survived last season, finishing in 22nd. Their slide from League One straight to the bottom end of League Two means the next boss has a big task on their hands.

Woodman’s time at Bromley was nothing short of incredible. He helped guide the Ravens to the Football League for the first time in their history, then went one step further in earning promotion to League One.

The 55-year-old was a big hit in just his second managerial role, and he would be backed to bring better fortunes to Broadfield Stadium if he was to be hired. If the Red Devils could convince Woodman to be their new manager, they can quickly start looking back up the League Two table.

League Two’s bottom side needs an injection of life, and Woodman has not written off a move to the club.

The big question is whether Wodman would be willing to drop down to League Two and take charge of the side sitting in a perilous position. His work with Bromley means he arguably should be aiming a little higher, but the connection between him and Crawley could sway him into taking charge of their rescue mission.

There is plenty of work to be done at Broadfield Stadium between now and the end of the season, but the former Bromley boss may see the challenge as one he wants to take on.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang has resigned

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

The FBI is investigating as Nvidia’s board holds urgent talks over Huang’s future.

Originally published November 12, 2026

Nvidia chief executive Jensen Huang has resigned following revelations that he had two extramarital affairs, plunging the chipmaker at the heart of the global AI boom into crisis.

The 63-year-old will be replaced by chief financial officer Colette Kress, who will serve as interim chief executive.

His exit comes after he admitted to having a relationship with a woman that US authorities believe was a Chinese spy. It also emerged on Wednesday that Huang had an affair with a senior Nvidia executive.

Nvidia shares were down more than 10 percent in pre-market trading, extending earlier losses after the announcement of Huang’s resignation.

Huang, who cofounded Nvidia in 1993, led it to become what was until recently the world’s most valuable company.

Huang’s departure marks a startling end to his more than 33-year tenure, during which Nvidia evolved from a small video-game chip company into the linchpin of the global AI infrastructure boom.

Huang has become one of the most recognizable figures in tech, as Nvidia’s semiconductors became integral to training and running AI systems such as OpenAI’s ChatGPT and Anthropic’s Claude.

Nvidia stock extended its pre-market decline after the company announced that Jensen Huang had resigned as chief executive.

Shares were down 10.2 percent in pre-market trading, a move that would wipe off more than $400 billion in market capitalization.

Google’s new Android XR glasses work with iOS, Made for iPhone certified

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Google today announced Android XR audio glasses called Intelligent Eyewear that notably support iOS with Made for iPhone certification.

Hands-on: Google and Samsung’s vision for Android XR glasses is 20/20

Intelligent Eyewear is made by Samsung with frame designs from Gentle Monster and Warby Parker. There are 30 unique designs in total, and the frames weigh 40-44 grams with a 12-megapixel ultra-wide camera.

Google’s Glasses companion client for iOS handles compute, including HDR processing, and saves images and videos (up to 90 seconds) to Apple Photos. The Made for iPhone certification comes into play for Wi-Fi Aware connectivity that allows for fast media transfers.

On the privacy front, there is a blinking white indicator light when you use the camera to take pictures or record clips that are saved to your library. If the light is covered or disabled, the glasses will stop recording, with Android XR running a hardware check when charging.

Gemini commands that use the camera, like visual search or translation, don’t trigger the light because those photos or videos are not saved anywhere, including Gemini History. Before that media reaches the cloud, Google redacts faces from video feeds and filters out bystander voices. This processing is done by the companion app. Additionally, that media isn’t used to train AI models or target ads.

Other key specifications include four microphones and two open-ear speakers. Besides voice commands with the “Hey Gemini” hotword, there is a touchpad that accepts taps and swipes. A physical on/off switch lets you wear Intelligent Eyewear as regular glasses.

On iOS, Gemini can make calls, send 1:1 messages (including via iMessage), and receive notifications.

Battery life is rated at nine hours on a single charge, while there’s a charging case that stores seven charges. Features include turn-by-turn Google Maps directions and real-time translation. On the streaming front, there’s support for Apple Music, Spotify, YouTube Music, and Audible.

Google’s Android XR glasses require an iPhone 13 Pro or newer.

Warby Parker Intelligent Eyewear is available in three frame designs, starting at $395 for optical and sun styles. Gentle Monster Intelligent Eyewear is available in the US and South Korea from $449.

Pre-orders open today with retail availability on December 10.

Jensen Huang is out as NVIDIA CEO

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

He has held the position since the company's founding.

On Thursday, NVIDIA's board of directors said that Huang had resigned his position as president and chief executive of the company, effective immediately. Huang also gave up his board seat. NVIDIA named Colette Kress, the company's current executive vice president and chief financial officer, as interim CEO.

"Kress will continue to serve as Executive Vice President and Chief Financial Officer while serving as Interim Chief Executive Officer until a permanent successor has been named," NVIDIA said. "The Board is initiating a search for a permanent Chief Executive Officer and will consider internal and external candidates."

As part of the shakeup, NVIDIA also appointed its first-ever chair of the board in Harvey Jones, who previously served as CEO and executive chairman of electronic design automation company Synopsys. "Colette has helped lead NVIDIA through a period of extraordinary growth and has a deep understanding of our business, customers, operations and financial position," Jones said in a statement. "She is supported by an experienced leadership team that has been instrumental in executing NVIDIA's strategy and advancing its technology roadmap. The Board has full confidence in Colette and the leadership team, and we expect the company's operations, customer commitments and product roadmap to continue without interruption."

Huang's departure comes days after NVIDIA's board said it would investigate Huang after he admitted to having an affair with a former tech executive accused of being a Chinese intelligence asset. The board said at the time it had "no reason to believe that either national security or NVIDIA secrets were compromised." The FBI is also investigating, according to President Donald Trump.

The New York Times later reported that Huang had also had an affair with an NVIDIA executive who reported to him, and that the two were seen "being affectionate" at a hotel during a company conference this year. A bystander who reportedly recorded the interaction was offered a nondisclosure agreement by Huang's security detail, according to the report.

Huang's downfall is a stunning turn for the company, which has become a linchpin for the AI industry. He has been CEO since co-founding the company in 1993. Since then, he has transformed NVIDIA from a 3D graphics company to an AI powerhouse worth more than $4 trillion.

As NVIDIA's stock price has exploded in recent years Huang has become an increasingly public face of the AI boom. He often appeared at events — usually wearing his signature black leather jacket — with fellow tech CEOs like Mark Zuckerberg and Satya Nadella. At a time when high profile AI researchers are raising the alarm about superintelligence, Huang has had a much more positive view on the technology. He argued against increased regulation of the industry and has said America should not restrict China's access to US-made chips.

Huang has frequently been seen with President Trump, and was one of several tech leaders to accompany him on a recent trip to China. Asked about recent revelations, the president said he believed Huang was "an honorable guy" and that he "probably has an excuse for it."

In a memo to NVIDIA employees earlier this week in which he apologized for his actions, Huang wrote that leading the company was "the privilege of my life" and that he was "committed to protecting NVIDIA and supporting America's national security." He added that he was "fully committed to NVIDIA" and would "work to earn back your confidence." But the CEO who once seemed untouchable won't get the chance to do so.

Jensen Huang resigns as Nvidia CEO after affairs and spy scandal

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

The move capped a remarkable fall from power for an executive who had been one of the faces of the generative artificial intelligence boom

Jensen Huang has resigned as CEO of Nvidia $NVDA, the company said Thursday, capping a remarkable fall from power for an executive who had been one of the faces of the generative artificial intelligence boom. CFO Colette Kress will serve as interim CEO, the company said, after revelations of multiple extramarital affairs by Huang, including one with a suspected Chinese spy. Huang also left the board, effective immediately, Nvidia said.

Nvidia’s board appointed Harvey C. Jones, a former CEO and executive chairman of Synopsys $SNPS, as its first-ever chair. The board is starting a search for a permanent CEO and will consider internal and external candidates. Kress will keep her CFO role while she serves as interim CEO.

"The Board has full confidence in Colette and the leadership team, and we expect the company's operations, customer commitments and product roadmap to continue without interruption," Jones said in a statement. Kress said in a statement that she is committed to building on the foundation Huang created over 33 years as Nvidia's founder and CEO.

Nvidia stock was down about 7% in Thursday morning trading. It began a sharp fall in after-hours trading Wednesday evening after reports that the board was meeting to consider ousting Huang.

The resignation follows reports of two extramarital affairs. Huang, 63, admitted to infidelity in a note sent to employees on Sunday. That relationship was with Alice Huang, a former executive at Singapore-based Megaspeed, a major Nvidia customer. U.S. intelligence officials suspect she is an agent of Beijing’s spy agencies. (Jensen Huang and Alice Huang are not related.)

The second affair was with Kimberly Powell, according to The Wall Street Journal and other outlets. Powell, who runs Nvidia's healthcare business, has not commented publicly.

Jensen Huang rejected claims that his actions put U.S. security or the chipmaker's confidential information at risk once the reports about Alice Huang surfaced. The Justice Department is heading several separate probes that touch on Alice Huang, according to The Journal. Megaspeed and Nvidia have denied wrongdoing in a U.S. probe of suspected chip smuggling into China. And China's Foreign Ministry has called the espionage allegation unsubstantiated.

Nvidia’s board opened an independent investigation on Sunday, overseen by a committee of independent directors. The company said Tuesday that the directors retained Joon H. Kim, a former acting U.S. attorney, and the law firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to conduct it. The board said it had no reason to believe national security or Nvidia secrets were compromised. Three House Republican committee chairs have asked Jensen Huang to appear for a private session with lawmakers on Nov. 18.

The company is set to report third-quarter earnings on Tuesday.

Could a nuclear blast save Earth from an asteroid? Good aim will mean everything

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Computer simulations reveal how an asteroid’s shape could affect efforts to push it off course with X-rays from a nearby nuclear blast.

A nuclear blast set off near a dangerous asteroid could, in theory, push it away from Earth. But if the asteroid has a lumpy, uneven shape, how hard it gets pushed depends on which side the blast hits.

In new computer tests, hitting one side of an oddly shaped asteroid instead of another gave it nearly five times as much push.

So a real defense mission could depend on knowing an asteroid’s shape before anyone chooses where to aim.

Nathan Moore, a physicist at Sandia National Laboratories, led the research. “We expected aim to matter,” he said. “What surprised us was how much.”

No asteroid was blasted for this work. The team ran every test in computer simulations, using the shapes of real asteroids.

In this kind of defense, a nuclear device would go off some distance from the asteroid, not on it.

The blast sends out a burst of X-rays that heats one side of the asteroid and turns part of its surface into vapor. That vapor rushes off into space, and the pressure it puts on the surface pushes the asteroid the other way.

The goal is to push the asteroid, not smash it. Breaking it apart could turn one big threat into smaller pieces that could still be deadly.

A nuclear blast is a different approach from crashing a spacecraft into an asteroid, which NASA’s DART mission tested in 2022.

Moore has worked on this question since a 2024 experiment at Sandia’s Z machine, a lab device that produces intense bursts of X-rays.

That experiment showed for the first time that X-rays could push a small piece of asteroid material.

Next, he wanted to know how a full-size asteroid would respond. Most researchers had treated asteroids as perfect spheres because that made the math easier, he said.

Real asteroids have irregular shapes that a perfect sphere cannot capture.

“You can make estimates that way, but asteroids come in all kinds of odd, lumpy shapes,” Moore said.

He found a way forward in a colleague’s work on how objects in space give off gas.

“I think we had an aha moment when we realized we could do this,” he said. “No more spheres. We would be able to model deflection for any asteroid.”

Moore built the new model by joining two computer programs that Sandia already used for very different jobs.

One usually simulates fast, high-energy events, such as experiments on the Z machine. The other usually handles weak forces that change slowly, like the drag the solar wind puts on a satellite.

In the new model, the first program works out how the X-rays heat the surface and turn part of it to gas.

The second follows that gas as it spreads across the asteroid’s real 3D shape, and it calculates the pressure on every part of the surface.

“The key innovation here was figuring out how to combine the two simulation approaches,” Moore said.

He also pointed to speed. “Our new algorithm is over 1,600 times faster, which was critical to this breakthrough,” he said. “With the old models, computers just couldn’t handle it.”

To use the model, the team loads in an asteroid’s shape, which can come from detailed 3D images taken by passing spacecraft.

They then set the size of the blast, how far away it goes off, and which side it hits.

The program maps how much of each part of the surface turns to vapor. From that map, it works out how hard the asteroid gets pushed and how fast it spins afterward.

The team tested the model on three real asteroids with very different shapes. Bennu is the asteroid NASA’s OSIRIS-REx spacecraft sampled in 2020. The other two were Itokawa and Golevka.

For each one, the researchers tried blasts at close range and farther away. They also changed which way the asteroid was facing when the X-rays arrived.

In the simulations, orienting Bennu at its most favorable angle, instead of its worst, gave it up to nearly twice as much push.

The difference was larger for the other two, at up to nearly four times for Itokawa and five times for Golevka.

In one simulation, the blast changed an asteroid’s speed by about 0.15 inch (3.9 millimeters) per second from one angle. From another angle, it was only about 0.03 inch (0.8 millimeter) per second.

The asteroid and the blast were the same both times, and only the side facing the X-rays was different.

The aim also affected how fast an asteroid spun after the blast. For one asteroid, the change in its spin rate varied 17-fold depending on which side faced the blast.

“Our study shows how important it is to aim carefully,” Moore said. “Pick the right side and you win. Guess wrong and it could be a bad day.”

To run the model, the team needs a fairly accurate picture of an asteroid’s shape from every side. In a real emergency, there might not be time to get one.

Placing a nuclear device at the right distance, on the right side of an asteroid speeding through space, would also be hard.

Another unknown is what happens to the whole asteroid.

“Right now, our computer model only considers breakup of rocks on the surface,” he said. “We can’t predict breakup of the entire asteroid body accurately yet.”

When the researchers applied the blast pressures to a loose, Itokawa-like pile of boulders, the rock thrown off was lopsided, just like the vapor.

Predicting whether a whole asteroid breaks apart will take more research and better modeling tools. An X-ray blast would set off many effects at once, and those effects could change one another.

That full calculation is what Moore and other scientists are working toward.

The full study was published in the journal Icarus.

Five Italy players at risk of suspension if booked against France

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Italy will be in Nations League action against France on Thursday evening, and five of Roberto Mancini’s players will need to avoid a booking if they are to remain eligible to feature against Belgium later this week.

Italy will take on France in San Siro at 19.45 GMT in their penultimate match of the Nations League group stage. The two nations were even at 1-1 when they met in Paris last month.

As things stand ahead of kick-off, France lead the group on 10 points after four matches, Italy are in second on seven points, Belgium are third on six and Turkey are last on zero.

Mancini’s matchday squad of 23 players has recently been confirmed: Honest Ahanor, Federico Gatti, Matteo Palma, Niccolo Pisilli, Alphadjo Cisse and Mattia Liberali have been sacrificed, but could still potentially feature against Belgium on Friday.

Goalkeepers: 1. Gianluigi Donnarumma, 12. Marco Carnesecchi, 13. Guglielmo Vicario

Defenders: 2. Michael Kayode, 3. Giorgio Scalvini, 5. Riccardo Calafiori, 6. Alessandro Bastoni, 11. Federico Dimarco, 14. Marco Palestra,  22. Daniele Ghilardi.

Midfielders: 4. Samuele Ricci, 8. Sandro Tonali, 10. Nicolò Fagioli, 15. Issa Doumbia, 16. Davide Frattesi, 17. Alessandro Romano, 18. Nicolo Barella

Forwards: 7. Sebastiano Esposito, 9. Pio Esposito, 19. Nicolò Cambiaghi, 20. Gianluca Scamacca, 21. Antonio Vergara, 23. Luca Koleosho.

In the Nations League, players earn one-match suspensions for every even number of yellow cards they earn, i.e. after every second, fourth and sixth booking.

As things stand, nine Italy players have been shown yellow cards during the first four group stage matches of the Nations League campaign. Five of those players are in the matchday squad against France.

Goalkeeper Gianluigi Donnarumma, centre-back Alessandro Bastoni, central midfielder Nicolo Fagioli, winger Antonio Vergara and centre-forward Gianluca Scamacca are the five Italy players at risk of suspension on Thursday evening.

Moise Kean, Daniel Maldini, Gianluca Mancini and Matteo Ruggeri have also been booked this Nations League campaign, but are not involved with the current Italy national team squad.

Only three France players have been booked so far this Nations League campaign, and fortunately for Zinedine Zidane, none of them will be in action against Italy on Thursday. The Bleus players currently on bookings are Manu Kone, Maxence Lacrois and Esteban Lepaul.

'The Acolyte' Star's Upcoming Fantasy Adaptation Officially Gets Stunning New Trailer

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

As 2026 winds down, the big-budget fantasy adaptations have only continued rolling in. Last month saw the release of LAIKA's long-gestating stop-motion rendition of Wildwood to overwhelming acclaim from both critics and audiences, while November has marked the arrival of the Indian epic Ramayana: Rise of a Legend, the first part of a duology that clocks in as the country's most expensive undertaking ever. On Christmas Day, HBO will also give the gift of its much-anticipated Harry Potter series. Even as the calendar flips over, the streak will stay alive with a much-anticipated, if controversial, epic starring The Acolyte lead, Amandla Stenberg.

On January 15, Children of Blood and Bone will finally get its time in the sun with Gina Prince-Bythewood's fantasy epic adaptation in theaters. Paramount has been slowly building up buzz for the star-studded film, based on one of the most popular and acclaimed YA novels of the past ten years from bestselling author Tomi Adeyemi. Last month, the cast, led by Thuso Mbedu, Damson Idris, Tosin Cole, and Stenberg, appeared at New York Comic Con to tease the painstaking work to bring the world of Orïsha to life, from the elaborate costumes to the dazzling effects and extensive fight training. Now, Zélie Adebola's (Mbedu) story is getting another look with two months remaining until release.

Paramount has shared a new trailer showcasing Children of Blood and Bone's magic, African mythology-inspired world in stunning fashion. At the center is Zélie, a young woman who witnessed the magic of her kingdom be violently ripped away by its ruthless king, Saran (Chiwetel Ejiofor), leaving her without a mother. Saran created a society where the magically-inclined divîners have no power, and the few allowed to survive are forced into lives of poverty and oppression. To restore what her people lost and save Orïsha, she and her brother join forces with the King's own son and daughter to oppose his reign of terror.

Four worlds and four completely different ideas of what a good life looks like. This is where you belong, what it would ask of you, and what you would get back in return.

You belong somewhere old enough that the mountains have names and somebody can still tell you who gave them. Middle-earth is for people who think the right thing is worth doing even when it will not be finished in their lifetime.

You want the sea, a crew, and absolutely nobody telling you where you are allowed to go. The Grand Line is the world for people whose main ambition in life is finding out what is over there.

You would rather be feared and useful than liked and useless. The Continent is a world where the monsters are the straightforward part of the job and the people are where it all gets complicated.

You want the myths to be real and loose in the actual world, and you want to find out at about thirteen that you were never anywhere near as ordinary as everybody kept telling you.

Alongside the main stars standing against King Saran, the fantasy epic also features Cynthia Erivo, Lashana Lynch, Zackary Momoh, Richard Mofe-Damijo, Regina King, Idris Elba, Viola Davis, and many more among its massive ensemble. All three of Mbedu, Davis, and Lynch also worked with Prince-Bythewood on her last film, The Woman King, in 2022. All the award-worthy firepower on Children of Blood and Bone is finally poised to get the ambitious fantasy adaptation across the finish line after years of the project languishing in development hell and changing hands from the Disney umbrella to Paramount. Despite co-writing the script, however, Adeyemi ultimately distanced herself from the production this summer, saying she would not continue "being hurt and attacked behind the scenes" and citing a rift with Stenberg specifically.

Children of Blood and Bone takes over theaters on January 15. Check out the new trailer in the player above.

SoFi Brings Its Bank-Issued Stablecoin to Polygon’s Payments Stack

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

SoFi Brings Its Bank-Issued Stablecoin to Polygon’s Payments Stack

SoFi is widening the reach of its digital dollar, bringing SoFiUSD, the first stablecoin issued by a U.S. national bank, onto Polygon’s payments infrastructure as banks and fintechs push stablecoins deeper into everyday money movement.

Polygon Labs said Thursday that SoFiUSD is now live on Polygon Open Money Stack, a payments layer that bundles fiat on- and off-ramps, embedded wallets and cross-chain settlement into a single integration.

The move adds Polygon alongside Ethereum and Solana, the networks SoFiUSD already runs on, and lets SoFi’s more than 17 million members buy, sell, hold, and convert the token on Polygon directly in the SoFi app, without opening a separate crypto wallet or manually bridging assets between chains.

“SoFi is well positioned to bring stablecoins into mainstream finance because it combines the regulatory foundation of a national bank with the reach of a major consumer and enterprise technology platform,” said Marc Boiron, CEO of Polygon Labs.

The expansion lands as stablecoins move beyond crypto trading into the plumbing used to settle card payments, pay businesses, and send money across borders.

On Polygon Open Money Stack, SoFiUSD joins USDC, USDT and PayPal’s PYUSD, and can connect to the wallets, banking rails, and local currencies businesses need for payouts, settlement and cross-border transfers, opening up uses well beyond consumer trading.

The token is redeemable one-to-one for U.S. dollars and issued by SoFi Bank, which is regulated by the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency.

SoFi launched SoFiUSD late last year across Ethereum and Solana as the first stablecoin from a U.S. national bank, and it already settles the company’s entire card program over Mastercard’s network. The firm has been building out a broader digital-money suite, recently launching SoFi Earn, a tokenized deposit that pays 4.40% yield and converts one-to-one into SoFiUSD.

For Polygon, the addition deepens a payments business that has become one of the network’s calling cards.

Polygon recently extended Open Money Stack to Solana, and the chain has processed more than $3 trillion in lifetime stablecoin transfer volume, according to the company. Visa and Mastercard support stablecoin settlement on Polygon, while Stripe uses it to route USDC payments for merchants in more than 150 countries.

SoFiUSD is a payment stablecoin, not a bank deposit, and is not FDIC- or SIPC-insured, bank-guaranteed or legal tender.

Hands-on: Google and Samsung’s vision for Android XR glasses is 20/20

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

While I acknowledge Meta’s role in bringing smart glasses to a mainstream audience, it has proven to be an irresponsible steward. Its “growth at all costs” mindset has actively poisoned people’s perception of the form factor. Under Meta’s watch, glasses have gained a creepy connotation before their utility has been fully realized.

Google’s second take on smart glasses is the exact opposite of what Meta has done. Every single aspect has been considered, and the result is worthy of being most people’s introduction to the form factor.

Officially, Android XR glasses are known as Gentle Monster and Warby Parker Intelligent Eyewear. The branding is a mouthful, and I’m not quite sure “Intelligent Eyewear” will take off. Something more colloquial, like “Google glasses” — glasses.google is the landing page — or “Samsung glasses,” will probably stick in the public consciousness.

That critique aside, the name reflects how Google wants Intelligent Eyewear to actually replace your existing glasses. Unlike the Ray-Ban trap that I frankly think Meta did not consider, Google’s offerings are not situational or activity-based devices like sunglasses. These are a “smart” upgrade to the assistive device some people have to wear.

As a wearable, smart glasses are a bit like smartwatches in that the platonic design is pretty well established and indistinguishable from traditional eyewear. Unlike watches, glasses are so visible on people’s faces that form overrides everything else.

Fortunately, the Samsung hardware I wore for an hour last week looks and feels like glasses, almost unremarkably so. I’d venture to say that audio-only glasses without a display are a solved design challenge. Google emphasized that, at 41 grams, this is the lightest pair of smart glasses on the market, and it worked to make sure the weight is distributed evenly.

The sides of the stems that face your head feature translucent plastic that lets you see the internal components. I liked that small design choice. You can control the glasses using voice commands, with Google debuting a new “Hey Gemini” hotword for the form factor. While “Hey Google” and “OK Google” remain available, the new one is meant to reflect how big a role Gemini plays on smart glasses.

There is also a touchpad on the right stem that accepts taps and swipes, while a button up top lets you take pictures and record video. Another way you’ll be able to control the glasses is with your Pixel Watch and Galaxy Watch. You can play/pause audio and enable Gemini using double-pinch and wrist gestures. Notably, gesture support is also coming to the Fitbit Air next year.

From its internal testing, Google tells me that which input method people use is pretty situational and ends up being natural. At the beginning of my demo, I favored the touchpad, but by the end, I was leaning toward voice commands.

When you’re capturing media that gets saved, there will always be a white blinking indicator light. There’s also another light in your field of vision that’s analogous to the camera and microphone privacy indicators in the top-right corner of Android phones. When I first put on the glasses, that light was distracting, but I quickly forgot about it by the end of my demo.

Talking to Intelligent Eyewear uses Gemini Live. Some prompts, like take a picture or play a song, are one-shot “chat” commands in phone parlance, but for the most part, you’re using Gemini’s back-and-forth conversation mode. Google heavily customized the Gemini Live model for glasses to reduce latency, shorten responses, and tightly integrate the camera.

Even compared to my last demo in May, the latency of voice commands has come a long way. That said, I need more time to see if it’s really fast enough to use as a primary interaction method for most commands.

Google built a number of privacy protections into Intelligent Eyewear that are clearly in response to the current perception of smart glasses. There are some interesting ideas here, starting with how Gemini queries that involve a picture (like a visual search) won’t be saved to your Gemini History, used for model training, or ad targeting. For example, if you ask Gemini to find out what kind of plant you’re looking at, that image won’t be accessible anywhere else.

Similarly, Google will blur all faces and filter out bystander voices before it gets uploaded to Gemini in the cloud. This on-device processing occurs on your phone.

Since that visual media isn’t being saved, the external indicator light won’t be active when you’re talking to Gemini. The LED is only on when photos or videos are being saved and can be reviewed later.

That indicator light features anti-tamper detection that prevents recording. Algorithms detect whether the LED is covered by stickers or markers, with this process featuring hardware verification when the glasses are in their charging case.

Google’s “Glasses” companion app, with Samsung having a near-identical version for Galaxy phones, is pretty similar to the version for Pixel Watch. Unlike the bloated “Meta AI” app, it’s just for settings rather than the very definition of a growth hack with a full chatbot. The difference is staggering, and something Google deserves a bit of credit for in this age of throwing AI into every crevice of day-to-day smartphone usage.

Under the hood, the glasses are offloading a lot of processing to your phone. This includes HDR pipelines and video stabilization. The 12MP camera takes pictures in a 4:3 aspect ratio, while you can record up to 90 seconds of video. Images are transferred immediately to your phone over Wi-Fi, while videos wait until the glasses are charging to preserve battery life. I am so excited by point-of-view photography and the instantaneous capture the glasses offer, and early impressions of camera quality are positive.

Another thing I want to test is how Intelligent Eyewear fares as always-available headphones. There are two open-ear speakers and four microphones, and Google is clearly very proud of the noise-cancellation work in place.

As someone who has been waiting for smart glasses since 2012, I cannot overstate my annoyance at how Meta allowed smart glasses to gain a scummy undertone, if not overtone. It should have been more proactive to societal issues. Meanwhile, Meta releasing glasses without a camera, thus removing the primary utility of real-world context, makes me deeply question its belief that this is the future.

Smart glasses that know what you’re interacting with in the world can be extremely useful. That always-available camera is a genuine physical advantage over smartphones. It unlocks new use cases and convenience that are just not possible with something that goes in your pocket.

Google took a long time to get back to glasses. From what I’ve used so far, the wait will be worth it. The hardware and designs are solid, while the software is chock-full of innovative, well-considered ideas.
